<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Fujita's freeness conjecture. If <i>X</i> is a smooth complex projective variety of dimension <i>n</i> and <i>L</i> is an ample line bundle, then $`K_X+mL`$ is globally generated for every integer $`m\geq n+1`$.

- **claim-sharp-adjoint-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ of dimension $n\geq 1$, and let $L$ be an ample line bundle on $X$. Then $$K_X+(n+1)L$$ is generated by its global sections.
  - Explanation: The adjoint bundle is globally generated under the stated smoothness, projectivity, ground-field and ampleness assumptions. The conclusion concerns nonvanishing at each point rather than separation.
- **claim-local-monomial-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-monomial-local)): A monomial order with fixed positive support can be computed in the Taylor ring at any smooth point of its open stratum. Its value is unchanged by replacing each positive-weight coordinate by a unit multiple or by changing the complementary zero-weight coordinates.
  - Explanation: Local Taylor computations recover the original order without turning a coefficient vanishing at the chosen point into an additional positive-weight contribution.
- **claim-snc-retraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-retraction)): Let $D_1,\ldots,D_N$ be effective divisors on $X$, and let $Y\to X$ be a log resolution of their union. Include all exceptional divisors in its SNC divisor, with components $E_j$. For any positive multiple $v$ of a prime-divisor order over $Y$, set $u_j=v(E_j)$. Then $$\label{eq:retraction} v(D_i)=\sum_j u_j\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{E_j}(D_i),\qquad A(v)\geq\sum_j u_jA(E_j).$$ If some $u_j$ is nonzero, the monomial order with these weights at the stratum containing the generic center of $v$ has the same orders on the $D_i$, no larger discrepancy, and a closed center on $X$ containing that of $v$.
  - Explanation: Retraction preserves orders on the resolved divisors and can lower discrepancy. Its closed center can enlarge, a feature needed when limits lose positive weights.
- **claim-closed-family-threshold-loci** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lct-family)): Let $V$ be a smooth complex projective variety, let $S$ be a complex algebraic variety, and let $\mathfrak a$ be an ideal on $V\times S$ locally given by algebraic families of generators. For a real number $c$, the locus $$\{(x,s):\mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_x(\mathfrak a_s)\leq c\}$$ is Zariski closed. Here $\mathfrak a_s$ denotes the ideal generated by the restricted generators, with no flatness requirement on its zero scheme.
  - Explanation: Analytic closedness and algebraic constructibility combine to give Zariski closedness. The statement uses restricted generators and does not require flatness of their zero schemes.
- **claim-rounded-kawamata-viehweg-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-kv)): Let $T$ be smooth and projective over $\mathbb C$. Let $\Delta$ be an effective rational SNC divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$, and let $D$ be an integral divisor. If $D-(K_T+\Delta)$ is nef and big, then $H^i(T,\mathcal O_T(D))=0$ for every $i>0$.
  - Explanation: The higher-cohomology vanishing supplies the surjectivity needed to lift a section from the equality divisor.
- **claim-birational-section-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent)): Let $\pi:T\to V$ be a proper birational morphism of smooth varieties, let $P_V$ be a line bundle on $V$, and let $C$ be an integral divisor on $T$ whose positive part is exceptional. A section of $\pi^*P_V+C$ defines a regular section of $P_V$ on $V$. If a prime $F$ on $T$ maps to a point $x$, the coefficient of $C$ on $F$ is zero, and the section is nonzero generically on $F$, then the descended section is nonzero at $x$.
  - Explanation: Exceptional positive corrections cannot introduce poles along nonexceptional primes. Generic nonvanishing on a prime over the point prevents the descended section from vanishing there.
- **claim-initial-exponent-count-and-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-min-initials)): The set $H_j$ has cardinality $N_j$, and representatives of its distinct exponents form a basis of $R_j$. There is a constant $C>0$, independent of $j$ and $s$, such that $$\label{eq:min-multiplicity} \mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)\leq Cj\qquad(0\ne s\in R_j).$$ Moreover $H_j+H_q\subset H_{j+q}$.
  - Explanation: Elimination counts section dimensions through distinct Taylor initials. Intersection with a fixed ample curve bounds multiplicities, while multiplication adds initial exponents.
- **claim-strict-ordinary-order-gap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-ordinary-gap)): If $P$ is not generated by its global sections at $x$, then $$\label{eq:min-gap} \liminf_{j\longrightarrow\infty} \frac{1}{jN_j}\sum_{\gamma\in H_j}\|\gamma\|_1 >\frac{n}{n+1}.$$
  - Explanation: The lattice lower bound becomes strict under hypothetical failure of generation. Its equality case supplies leading forms whose threshold and vanishing argument already produce nonvanishing.
- **claim-discrepancy-multiplicity-order-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-min-order-bound)): If the center of a test $v$ contains $x$, then every nonzero section $s$ satisfies $$\label{eq:min-order-bound} v(s)\leq A(v)\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s).$$ In particular, for $s\in R_m$ one has $0\leq v(s)/A(v)\leq Cm$, with $C$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:min-initials" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:min-initials">7</a>.
  - Explanation: A local threshold estimate bounds normalized section orders by ordinary multiplicity. This controls the lists used in the attainment argument.
- **claim-uniform-exponential-sublevels** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-min-coercivity)): Suppose that $P$ is not generated at $x$. There are numbers $0<t_0<n+1$, $c>0$, $0<\delta<1$, $0<a_*\leq a^*<\infty$, and an integer $m_0$ such that, for every $m\geq m_0$ and every $t\in[t_0,n+1]$, $$\label{eq:min-uniform-bounds} c\leq f_{m,t}(x)\leq1-\delta.$$ Moreover, any pair in <a href="#eq:min-objective" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-objective">[eq:min-objective]</a> centered with image containing $x$ and satisfying $F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s)\leq1-\delta/2$ has $$\label{eq:min-discrepancy-bounds} a_*\leq A(v)\leq a^*.$$ All these constants are independent of $m$ and $t$ in the indicated ranges.
  - Explanation: The ordinary-order gap gives an objective below its limiting value at zero scale. Low-order jets prevent escape to large discrepancy, and the order bound prevents escape to zero discrepancy.
- **claim-closed-normalized-order-realizability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-min-flags)): Fix $m$ with $N_m>0$ and a decreasing list $\alpha_1\geq\cdots\geq\alpha_{N_m}\geq0$ of rational numbers. The set of points $z\in X$ for which some ordered basis and some prime-divisor order $v$, with $z\in c_X(v)$, satisfy $$\label{eq:min-rational-list} \frac{v(s_i)}{A(v)}\geq\alpha_i \qquad(1\leq i\leq N_m)$$ is Zariski closed. There is also a closed incidence locus in the product of $X$ with the complete flag variety of $R_m$ recording realizability by bases adapted to a given flag.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous normalized-order constraints become a threshold condition for a sum of powers of flag base ideals. Properness of the flag variety then preserves closedness under projection.
- **claim-attained-real-weight-minimum** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-minimizers)): Suppose that $P$ is not generated at $x$. For the constants and ranges in Lemma <a href="#lem:min-coercivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:min-coercivity">10</a>, $f_{m,t}(x)$ satisfies <a href="#eq:min-uniform-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-uniform-bounds">[eq:min-uniform-bounds]</a> and is attained by an ordered basis of $R_m$ and a nonzero real-weight monomial test on a projective log resolution of the divisors of that basis. Every minimizing test has $$a_*\leq A(v)\leq a^*.$$ More generally the same bounds hold eventually along every minimizing sequence. In particular, the positive lower bound $a_*$ is independent of $m\geq m_0$ and $t\in[t_0,n+1]$.
  - Explanation: Nested closed flag loci select a single basis before a resolution is fixed. Retraction and compactness of normalized weights then realize the limiting objective.
- **claim-very-general-specialization-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-specialization)): For fixed $m$ with $N_m>0$, let $W\subset X$ be a closed irreducible subvariety containing $x$. At a very general closed point $y\in W$, one has, for every $t>0$, $$\label{eq:min-specialization} f_{m,t}(y)\geq f_{m,t}(x).$$
  - Explanation: Avoiding proper closed realizability loci ensures that every rational list realized at the chosen point is realized at the original point. The exceptional loci do not depend on the positive parameter.
- **claim-universal-point-centers** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-point-center)): Suppose that $P$ is not generated at $x$. There is a real number $0<t<n+1$ such that, for arbitrarily large integers $m$, the infimum $f_{m,t}(x)$ is attained and every minimizing monomial pair has center exactly $\{x\}$.
  - Explanation: A fixed parameter works along arbitrarily large degrees. Uniform restriction growth contradicts the tangential moment bound for any positive-dimensional minimizing center, even when models and centers vary.
- **claim-uniform-restriction-image-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-restrictions)): For each $1\le d\le n$, there are constants $C_d$ and $H_d$, depending only on $(X,L,k,d)$, such that $$\label{eq:uniform-restrictions} \bigl(d!I_W(h)\bigr)^{1/d}\ge h-C_d \qquad(h\ge H_d)$$ for every integral $d$-dimensional closed subvariety $W\subset X$.
  - Explanation: Finite projection and independent ambient forms give a lower bound for the image of restriction. The constants remain independent of the subvariety's degree and singularities.
- **claim-rational-incidence-preserving-support** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-support)): Let $\mathcal C$ be a finite family of rational polytopes in $\mathbb R^N$, and let $q$ belong to their union. Suppose that a vector $b\in\mathbb R_{>0}^N$ satisfies $$b\cdot q=1, \qquad b\cdot z\leq 1 \quad\text{for every }z\in C\text{ whenever }q\in C\in\mathcal C.$$ Fix an open neighborhood $V$ of $b$. There are rational vectors $q^0$ arbitrarily close to $q$ and $b^0\in V\cap\mathbb Q_{>0}^N$ such that* 1. *$q^0\in C$ if and only if $q\in C$, for each $C\in\mathcal C$;* 2. *$b^0\cdot q^0=1$, and $b^0\cdot z\leq1$ on every $C\in\mathcal C$ containing $q^0$.
  - Explanation: Rational approximation takes place inside the intersection of the supporting faces. A rational feasible functional then preserves positivity, incidence and supporting inequalities.
- **claim-local-isolated-discrepancy-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-isolated-equality)): Every component of $S$ maps to $x$ and has coefficient $a_F$ in $B$. Every other prime in the SNC support meeting $S$ has coefficient strictly less than its log discrepancy.
  - Explanation: The ideal perturbation forces equality profiles to agree in every coordinate. Strict inequalities are required only on components meeting the reduced equality divisor.
- **claim-all-higher-adjoint-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-all-powers)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $K_X+mL$ is globally generated for every integer $m\geq n+1$.
  - Explanation: Applying the sharp theorem to a product with projective space and restricting to a fiber gives generation for every higher adjoint power, without requiring generation of the ample bundle itself.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Adjoint bundle and section spaces** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Write $$P=K_X+(n+1)L,\qquad R_j=H^0(X,jL),\qquad N_j=\dim R_j.$$
- **Closed centers** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): All centers are *closed* centers.
- **Divisorial log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a prime divisor $F$ on a smooth birational model $\pi:Y\to X$, the log discrepancy is $$A(F)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(K_{Y/X}).$$
- **Orders of sections and ideals** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Orders of sections mean orders of their effective zero divisors; orders of ideals mean the minimum of the orders of local generators.
- **Weighted monomial order and discrepancy** ([source](#eq:monomial-discrepancy)): The order of a germ is the least weight of a nonzero monomial in their local equations, with coefficients in the remaining coordinates, and $$\label{eq:monomial-discrepancy} A(v)=\sum_{j=1}^r u_jA(E_j).$$
- **Local log canonical threshold** ([source](#eq:lct-formula)): For a coherent ideal $\mathfrak a$ on a smooth variety, its local log canonical threshold at $x$ is computed on a log resolution by $$\label{eq:lct-formula} \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_x(\mathfrak a)= \min_{x\in\pi(F),\,\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(\mathfrak a)>0} \frac{A(F)}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(\mathfrak a)}.$$
- **Ordinary Taylor initial exponents** ([source](#sec:min-ordinary)): Let $$H_j=\{\operatorname{in}(s):0\ne s\in R_j\} \subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^n$$ be the set of initial exponents.
- **Exponential objective and its infimum** ([source](#eq:min-objective)): For $N_m>0$, an ordered basis $\mathbf s=(s_1,\ldots,s_{N_m})$ of $R_m$, a nonzero monomial test $v$, and $t>0$, put $$\label{eq:min-objective} F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s) =\frac1{N_m}\sum_{i=1}^{N_m} \exp\!\left(\frac{A(v)}t-\frac{v(s_i)}m\right), \qquad f_{m,t}(z)=\inf_{\substack{v,\mathbf s\\z\in c_X(v)}} F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s).$$
- **Restriction image dimension** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For an integral $d$-dimensional closed subvariety $W\subset X$, put $$I_W(h)=\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(R_h\longrightarrow H^0(W,hL\|_W)\bigr).$$
- **Normalized section-order polytopes** ([source](#eq:order-polytopes)): For every component $Z$ of an SNC stratum whose closure has image containing $x$, let $J(Z)$ be its set of crossing components and form the polytope $$C_Z=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{z_j:j\in J(Z)\}. \label{eq:order-polytopes}$$
- **Equality divisor** ([source](#eq:equality-divisor-definition)): Let $S$ be the reduced sum of the primes in the chosen SNC support satisfying all three conditions $$x\in\rho(F),\qquad \left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(D_i)}{a_F}\right)_i=q^0, \qquad r_F=a_F. \label{eq:equality-divisor-definition}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-adjoint-twisting-threshold | m | ≥ | $n+1$ | Integer adjoint powers in the stated range. |
| q-multiplicity-linear-bound | \mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s) | ≤ | $Cj$ | Nonzero sections in each graded section space. |
| q-strict-normalized-ordinary-gap | \liminf_{j\longrightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{jN_j}\sum_{\gamma\in H_j}\|\gamma\|_1 | > | $\frac{n}{n+1}$ | Asymptotic lower limit at the hypothetical base point. |
| q-discrepancy-normalized-section-bound | v(s) | ≤ | $A(v)\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)$ | All nonzero sections under the closed-center condition. |
| q-uniform-objective-upper-bound | f_{m,t}(x) | ≤ | $1-\delta$ | Uniform large-degree and parameter ranges supplied by coercivity. |
| q-uniform-objective-lower-bound | f_{m,t}(x) | ≥ | $c$ | Uniform indicated ranges; positivity comes from the supply of low-order jets. |
| q-sublevel-discrepancy-lower-bound | A(v) | ≥ | $a_*$ | Uniform in degree and parameter in the indicated ranges. |
| q-sublevel-discrepancy-upper-bound | A(v) | ≤ | $a^*$ | Uniform upper discrepancy bound for the same regime. |
| q-uniform-restriction-image-bound | \bigl(d!I_W(h)\bigr)^{1/d} | ≥ | $h-C_d$ | Degrees beyond the uniform threshold; constants depend only on the ambient data and dimension. |
| q-tangential-moment-upper-bound | T_m | ≤ | $\frac dt$ | Differentiate for the fixed finite basis and fixed degree. |
| q-supporting-coefficient-budget | m\sum_i b_i | < | $n+1$ | The fixed parameter lies strictly below the adjoint threshold. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-monomial-computation** [claim](#stmt-lem-monomial-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-monomial-local): Use primary monomial ideals and faithful flatness of completion to preserve the order under the permitted coordinate changes. -> Taylor computation independent of the permitted local choices.
- **step-snc-retraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-retraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-retraction): Read divisor orders from resolved monomials and compare discrepancies with the reduced SNC boundary. -> Monomial test preserving divisor orders with no larger discrepancy and an enlarged closed center.
- **step-threshold-family-closedness** [claim](#stmt-lem-lct-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lct-family): Combine analytic semicontinuity with generic-resolution stratification and constructibility. -> Zariski closed threshold locus.
- **step-vanishing-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-kv) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Use the cited rounding formulation of Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing. -> Higher-cohomology vanishing available for restriction sequences.
- **step-section-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent): Check nonnegative orders on nonexceptional primes and then use normality; detect nonvanishing by the prime over the marked point. -> Regular descended section nonzero at the marked point.
- **step-ordinary-initial-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-min-initials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-min-initials): Eliminate initial coefficients, bound multiplicity by intersection with a curve, and use multiplicativity of the ordering. -> Initial-exponent cardinality, multiplicity bound and additive semigroup inclusion.
- **step-ordinary-equality-lift** [claim](#stmt-prop-ordinary-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ordinary-gap): Analyze lattice equality, construct leading forms with a strict restricted threshold, and lift from the point blowup. -> Strict asymptotic ordinary-order gap.
- **step-normalized-order-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-min-order-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-min-order-bound): Use local integrability from Weierstrass preparation and then compute the threshold on a log resolution. -> Section order bounded by discrepancy times multiplicity.
- **step-uniform-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-min-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-min-coercivity): Use the ordinary-order gap for an upper sublevel bound, low-order jets for an upper discrepancy bound, and the normalized-order estimate for a lower discrepancy bound. -> Objective and discrepancy bounds uniform in the indicated degree and parameter ranges.
- **step-flag-realizability** [claim](#stmt-lem-min-flags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-min-flags): Encode simultaneous inequalities by a sum of powers of flag base ideals and apply threshold closedness and proper projection. -> Closed incidence and point realizability loci.
- **step-minimum-attainment** [claim](#stmt-prop-minimizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-minimizers): Choose a flag from nested closed loci, fix an adapted basis and its resolution, retract witnesses, and take a normalized weight limit. -> Attained minimum with the same uniform discrepancy bounds.
- **step-specialization-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-specialization): Exclude proper closed realizability loci and approximate normalized section-order lists from below. -> Comparison of infima at a very general point simultaneously for positive parameters.
- **step-uniform-restriction-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-restrictions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-restrictions): Project finitely to projective space, select independent ambient forms, multiply by base monomials, and cover residue classes by globally generated twists. -> Restriction-image lower bound independent of the moving subvariety.
- **step-point-center-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-prop-point-center) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Compare the tangential first-variation upper bound with slice growth, discrete Brunn–Minkowski and the limiting moment lower bound. -> Every minimizing pair has point center in the selected arbitrarily large degrees.
- **step-rational-supporting-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-support): Approximate inside the intersection of supporting faces and then within the rational feasible polyhedron for the functional. -> Rational profile and functional preserving exactly the indexed polytope incidence.
- **step-isolated-equality** [claim](#stmt-lem-isolated-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-isolated-equality): Extract the rational profile, principalize the isolating ideal and use strict positivity to make equality of the minimum rigid. -> Reduced equality divisor over the point with strict neighboring discrepancy inequalities.
- **step-final-adjoint-lift** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:final-lift): Apply rounded vanishing, restrict a constant fiber value using the locally effective correction, lift it and descend. -> Section of the adjoint bundle nonzero at the hypothetical base point, yielding the contradiction.
- **step-all-adjoint-powers** [claim](#stmt-cor-all-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-all-powers): Apply the sharp theorem to a product with projective space and restrict to a fiber. -> Global generation for every integer power in the corollary's range.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-adjoint-global-generation**: The specified adjoint bundle is generated by its global sections under the smooth complex projective and ample-bundle hypotheses.
- **invariant-attained-exponential-minimum**: Under the hypothetical failure of generation, the exponential infimum is attained by a basis and a nonzero real-weight monomial test, with uniform discrepancy bounds in the stated parameter ranges.
- **invariant-universal-point-center**: A fixed parameter below the adjoint threshold admits arbitrarily large degrees in which every minimizing monomial pair has exactly the prescribed point as its center.
- **invariant-isolated-discrepancy-equality**: The constructed reduced equality divisor maps to the prescribed point, and every other component of the specified crossing support that meets it has strictly smaller boundary coefficient than log discrepancy.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-weaken-geometric-hypotheses**: Apply the adjoint-generation conclusion to singular varieties or to a line bundle assumed merely nef. -> The theorem supplies no conclusion under these weakened hypotheses; its discrepancy and positivity arguments use the stated geometric assumptions.
- **anti-very-ampleness-conclusion**: Treat adjoint global generation as a certificate of separation of points or tangent directions. -> The conclusion supplies generation by global sections, which does not establish the stronger separation properties.
- **anti-arbitrary-degree-point-centers**: Assume that every minimizer has point center in every degree and for every positive parameter. -> The point-center proposition chooses a parameter and establishes its assertion in arbitrarily large degrees; it does not assert the proposed unrestricted regime.
- **anti-global-boundary-control**: Promote discrepancy control near the equality divisor to a globally log canonical boundary. -> The strict comparison is restricted to other primes in the specified crossing support meeting the equality divisor.
- **anti-finite-search-attainment-certificate**: Use a finite numerical search over bases and weights to certify attainment of the geometric infimum. -> Attainment relies on a projective intersection argument followed by a limiting construction on a fixed resolution; numerical agreement does not certify those existence steps.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The adjoint-generation theorem and its minimizing-center construction are not finite constructive results. Attainment uses geometric compactness: "The subsets are nested, so projectivity gives a flag in their intersection." The center argument also takes asymptotic limits: "Only now let $l$ tend to infinity." Finite auxiliary computations would not decide these existence and asymptotic proof obligations or certify global generation for arbitrary smooth complex projective varieties.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Global generation is an existence property of sections of an adjoint line bundle. The paper does not supply a software interface or an effective procedure for computing those sections from a representation of the variety. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local Taylor calculation concerns weighted orders on a birational model. Its coordinate invariance does not provide a geometric representation, rendering operation, or simulation update rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Closedness of threshold loci supplies a mathematical existence and specialization argument. It does not specify how a symbolic tool should construct the loci or compute their defining equations. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Elimination of Taylor coefficients gives a basis-selection mechanism when section expansions are already available. The paper does not provide an effective input representation, a termination bound for obtaining the required expansions, or a complete algorithm for producing the section spaces. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Initial exponents encode section-space dimensions and interact with multiplication. These mathematical sets suggest a possible representation of known section data, but the paper does not define storage, update, or query operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted sums used in the center argument are mathematical quantities evaluated for a fixed valuation and reference degree. Their finiteness alone supplies neither a hardware workload contract nor numerical precision requirements for acceleration. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The descent result establishes regularity and nonvanishing of sections under a birational morphism. It provides no security property, computational hardness assumption, or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exponential objective varies bases of section spaces and monomial valuations. The existence proof uses projective flag varieties and compactness, rather than a learning model or a training procedure with a computational convergence guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rational supporting-data argument preserves polytope incidence and supporting inequalities through rational approximation. This is relevant mathematical structure for constrained optimisation, but the paper does not give a finite solver contract for obtaining the original minimizing profile or choosing a certified approximation neighborhood. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The additive behavior of initial exponents concerns products of sections using common local frames. It supplies no protocol for coordinating independent processes or reconciling independently generated state. |
| other (algebraic geometry) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper constructs a rational boundary with an isolated discrepancy-equality divisor and uses cohomological vanishing to lift a section. This directly contributes to algebraic geometry, but it is not a finite engineering construction: obtaining the minimizing pair and choosing the sufficiently large degree depend on existence and asymptotic arguments. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-geometric-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The adjoint-generation conclusion is restricted to smooth complex projective varieties and ample line bundles. Extensions to singular varieties, other ground fields, or merely nef bundles are not established here. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to a singular variety.', 'Replace ampleness by nefness.', 'Transfer the conclusion to positive characteristic.']
- **limit-connectedness-and-zero-dimension** (edge_case): Connectedness fixes notation rather than imposing an essential restriction. The positive-dimensional theorem must be distinguished from the separately stated zero-dimensional case. Blocked use: ['Reject a disconnected variety solely because the theorem is written for a connected variety.', 'Apply the positive-dimensional discrepancy argument verbatim in dimension zero.']
- **limit-generation-versus-separation** (formal_scope): Global generation guarantees pointwise nonvanishing and does not establish separation of points, tangent directions, or very ampleness. Blocked use: ['Infer an embedding from the global-generation conclusion.', 'Claim tangent separation from the sharp freeness bound.']
- **limit-sharp-twisting-threshold** (counterexample_scope): The twisting threshold cannot be lowered uniformly; the projective-space example supplied by the paper obstructs that strengthening. Blocked use: ['Lower the adjoint twisting factor uniformly under the same hypotheses.']
- **limit-ample-bundle-section-assumption** (hypothesis): Ampleness does not supply global generation or even a nonzero section of the original ample bundle. Higher adjoint powers therefore require the corollary's argument rather than an unsupported tensoring argument. Blocked use: ['Assume the original ample bundle has a nonzero section.', 'Deduce higher adjoint generation by tensoring with an assumed globally generated original bundle.']
- **limit-adapted-monomial-coordinates** (hypothesis): The monomial discrepancy calculation requires coordinates adapted to the entire relative canonical divisor and the prescribed crossing support. The zero test is excluded. Blocked use: ['Compute discrepancy using arbitrary coordinate divisors without checking adaptation.', 'Admit the all-zero weight test.', 'Replace coefficient vanishing in complementary coordinates by additional positive weight.']
- **limit-retraction-center-enlargement** (formal_scope): Retraction preserves the resolved divisor orders and does not increase discrepancy, but its closed center may enlarge. It does not generally preserve the center exactly. Blocked use: ['Replace containment of centers by equality.', 'Assume retraction preserves discrepancy exactly.', 'Use the retracted test without checking that some weight remains nonzero.']
- **limit-threshold-family-contract** (hypothesis): Closedness applies to the stated algebraic families of generators on a smooth complex projective variety. Fiber ideals are defined by restricted generators, not by an assumed flat zero scheme. Blocked use: ['Transfer the closedness assertion to arbitrary nonalgebraic parameter dependence.', 'Replace the specified fiber ideal by a different scheme-theoretic construction without checking agreement.', 'Impose flatness as a necessary hypothesis of this lemma.']
- **limit-threshold-degenerate-ideals** (edge_case): The unit ideal and zero ideal have separate threshold conventions. Neither may be treated as an ordinary finite positive threshold case. Blocked use: ['Assign the same threshold to the unit ideal and zero ideal.', 'Evaluate a minimum over positive divisor orders without handling the unit ideal separately.']
- **limit-conditional-asymptotic-minimization** (formal_scope): The strict ordinary-order gap and uniform minimizer bounds belong to the hypothetical failure-of-generation argument. Their degree and parameter restrictions cannot be discarded. Blocked use: ['Assert the strict ordinary-order gap unconditionally.', 'Apply the uniform minimizer bounds in arbitrary small degrees or outside their stated parameter ranges.', 'Interpret hypothetical base-point minimizers as data supplied for every actual variety.']
- **limit-attainment-not-finite-search** (nonconstructive): Attainment selects a flag through projectivity and passes to a limit of normalized weights. This argument does not provide a finite global search procedure over varying bases and models. Blocked use: ['Treat a finite numerical search as a certificate of geometric attainment.', 'Declare a finite search complete without a separate effective representation and stopping argument.']
- **limit-very-general-specialization** (nonconstructive): Specialization is asserted at a very general closed point for a fixed degree. A chosen arbitrary point, or a simultaneous choice across all degrees, is not supplied by the statement. Blocked use: ['Apply the specialization comparison at an arbitrary closed point.', 'Assume the point selected for the main variation automatically supplies specialization in every auxiliary degree.']
- **limit-restriction-image-not-surjectivity** (formal_scope): The uniform estimate concerns the image of ambient restriction maps on integral subvarieties. It does not establish surjectivity or cover nonintegral subvarieties without an additional argument. Blocked use: ['Replace restriction-image dimension by the dimension of the entire target section space.', 'Assume every restriction map is surjective.', 'Extend the integral-subvariety estimate directly to reducible or nonreduced schemes.']
- **limit-point-center-regime-and-limits** (formal_scope): Universal point centers occur for a selected parameter in arbitrarily large degrees. The proof differentiates at a fixed basis and takes the degree limit before refining the partition. Blocked use: ['Assert point centers for every degree and every positive parameter.', 'Exchange the partition and degree limits without justification.', 'Require an unproved perturbation size uniform across varying degrees.']
- **limit-rational-support-preservation** (approximation): Rational approximation must preserve polytope incidence, supporting inequalities, and strict positivity. Unconstrained coordinate rounding does not satisfy this construction. Blocked use: ['Round the minimizing profile independently in each coordinate.', 'Allow a supporting coefficient to vanish.', 'Ignore polytopes excluded by the original profile.']
- **limit-local-boundary-control** (formal_scope): The isolated discrepancy comparison controls components meeting the equality divisor. It does not establish global log canonicity or global effectiveness of the correction divisor. Blocked use: ['Promote the neighboring discrepancy inequalities to a globally log canonical boundary.', 'Require global effectiveness of the correction as a consequence of the local construction.']
- **limit-rounded-vanishing-preconditions** (hypothesis): The lifting argument requires the stated smooth projective setting, an integral divisor, an effective rational crossing boundary with fractional coefficients, and a nef and big difference. Blocked use: ['Invoke the vanishing input without bigness.', 'Apply the rounding argument to an unchecked nonintegral divisor or crossing support.', 'Use a boundary with coefficients outside the specified fractional range.']
- **limit-reduced-equality-locus** (hypothesis): The restriction of the morphism through the prescribed point uses the reduced equality divisor. Set-theoretic mapping of a nonreduced replacement would not establish the same scheme-theoretic factorization. Blocked use: ['Replace the reduced equality divisor by a nonreduced thickening without checking factorization through the point.']
- **limit-descent-and-nonvanishing** (hypothesis): Regular descent requires an exceptional positive correction. Nonvanishing at the point additionally requires a prime over that point with zero correction coefficient and generic nonvanishing of the lifted section. Blocked use: ['Descend a section whose correction allows poles on nonexceptional primes.', 'Infer pointwise nonvanishing solely from existence of a section upstairs.', 'Omit the zero correction coefficient on the chosen prime.']
- **limit-finite-certification-scope** (nonconstructive): Finite auxiliary checks cannot certify the complete geometric existence and asymptotic argument. No general finite procedure or computational complexity for producing generating sections is specified in the supplied corpus. Blocked use: ['Treat agreement on finite examples as a proof of the general adjoint-generation theorem.', 'Publish an invented runtime or storage bound for constructing the global sections.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Fujita's freeness conjecture. If <i>X</i> is a smooth complex projective variety of dimension <i>n</i> and <i>L</i> is an ample line bundle, then $`K_X+mL`$ is globally generated for every integer $`m\geq n+1`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ of dimension $n\geq 1$, and let $L$ be an ample line bundle on $X$. |
| [stmt-lem-monomial-local](#stmt-lem-monomial-local) | lemma | `lem:monomial-local` | 2 | A monomial order with fixed positive support can be computed in the Taylor ring at any smooth point of its open stratum. |
| [stmt-lem-retraction](#stmt-lem-retraction) | lemma | `lem:retraction` | 3 | Let $D_1,\ldots,D_N$ be effective divisors on $X$, and let $Y\to X$ be a log resolution of their union. |

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

We use additive notation for line bundles. Fujita’s freeness conjecture (Fujita 1987) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[fujita1987](#ref-fujita1987)] predicts that the canonical bundle becomes globally generated after twisting by $n+1$ copies of an arbitrary ample line bundle on a smooth projective $n$-fold. The ample bundle itself need not have any nonzero sections. We prove the following precise form.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ of dimension $n\geq 1$, and let $L$ be an ample line bundle on $X$. Then $$K_X+(n+1)L$$ is generated by its global sections.*

</div>

The connectedness assumption only fixes notation: the assertion applies to each connected component. In dimension zero every line bundle is globally generated.

The exponent is sharp: for $X=\mathbb P^n$ and $L=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^n}(1)$, the bundle $K_X+(n+1)L$ is trivial, whereas $K_X+nL$ has no nonzero sections. Global generation asks for a nonvanishing section at each point; it does not assert separation of points or tangent directions, or the very-ampleness part of Fujita’s conjecture. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also implies that $K_X+mL$ is globally generated for every integer $m\geq n+1$, even when $L$ is not globally generated; see Corollary <a href="#cor:all-powers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:all-powers">18</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and methods

For curves, Riemann–Roch gives the sharp bound. The surface case follows from Reider’s theorem, and the threefold, fourfold, and fivefold cases were proved by Ein–Lazarsfeld, Kawamata, and Ye–Zhu, respectively (Reider 1988; Ein and Lazarsfeld 1993; Kawamata 1997; Ye and Zhu 2020) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[reider1988](#ref-reider1988), [einlazarsfeld1993](#ref-einlazarsfeld1993), [kawamata1997](#ref-kawamata1997), [yezhu2020](#ref-yezhu2020)]. In arbitrary dimension, Angehrn–Siu obtained generation for $m\geq (n^2+n+2)/2$ (Angehrn and Siu 1995) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[angehrnsiu1995](#ref-angehrnsiu1995)]. Helmke developed multiplicity estimates for the centers appearing in the vanishing argument (Helmke 1997, 1999) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[helmke1997](#ref-helmke1997), [helmke1999](#ref-helmke1999)]; Heier combined these estimates with the Angehrn–Siu method to obtain a bound of order $n^{4/3}$ (Heier 2002) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[heier2002](#ref-heier2002)]. Ghidelli–Lacini subsequently obtained the bound $m\geq n(\log\log n+2.34)$ for $n\geq2$ (Ghidelli and Lacini 2024, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ghidellilacini2024](#ref-ghidellilacini2024)]. Han’s recent preprint gives $m\geq\lceil C_0n\rceil$, where $C_0=1.77629988\ldots$ (Han 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[han2026](#ref-han2026)]. Chan announced a proof of the stronger intersection-number formulation in (Chan 2024, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[chan2024](#ref-chan2024)]; that preprint was withdrawn after the local extension argument did not guarantee the required vanishing order.

The common vanishing strategy constructs controlled singularities at the prescribed point, reduces a suitable center to a point, and then lifts a nonzero fiber value (Ein and Lazarsfeld 1993; Kawamata 1997) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[einlazarsfeld1993](#ref-einlazarsfeld1993), [kawamata1997](#ref-kawamata1997)]. A recurring difficulty is that a positive-dimensional center can itself be singular, and cutting it down costs positivity. Angehrn–Siu used movement to a smooth point and semicontinuity. Fujita estimated ranks of restriction maps to the exceptional divisor of a point blowup, while Helmke bounded the multiplicities of log canonical centers (Angehrn and Siu 1995; Fujita 1993; Helmke 1997, 1999) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[angehrnsiu1995](#ref-angehrnsiu1995), [fujita1993](#ref-fujita1993), [helmke1997](#ref-helmke1997), [helmke1999](#ref-helmke1999)]. Our argument controls a minimizing center by comparing the growth of images of restriction maps with a tangential first variation. The estimate is uniform over all integral subvarieties of a fixed dimension, including singular subvarieties of unbounded degree. The final lift uses the local discrepancy-and-vanishing mechanism of (Fujita 1993, sec. 1(1.6)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[fujita1993](#ref-fujita1993)]; the minimizing construction produces the configuration to which it applies.

The convex viewpoint has a related ancestry. Flag valuations, initial-exponent semigroups, and their relation to the dimensions of section spaces appear in Okounkov’s work (Okounkov 2003) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[okounkov2003](#ref-okounkov2003)] and are developed systematically by Lazarsfeld–Mustaţă and Kaveh–Khovanskii (Lazarsfeld and Mustaţă 2009; Kaveh and Khovanskii 2012) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[lazarsfeldmustata2009](#ref-lazarsfeldmustata2009), [kavehkhovanskii2012](#ref-kavehkhovanskii2012)]. Boucksom–Chen study the asymptotic distributions of filtration jumps (Boucksom and Chen 2011) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[boucksomchen2011](#ref-boucksomchen2011)], and Blum–Jonsson prove attainment for valuative log-canonical and stability thresholds of ample bundles (Blum and Jonsson 2020, Theorem E) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[blumjonsson2020](#ref-blumjonsson2020)]. The exponential average considered here is a different finite-degree objective, whose attainment is proved in Section <a href="#sec:min-exponential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:min-exponential">4</a>. The center argument uses elementary counts of initial exponents and the Brunn–Minkowski inequality directly.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes the discrepancy normalization and the threshold and vanishing results used below. Fix a point $x$ and suppose that the adjoint bundle is not generated there. An equality-case argument first gives a strict asymptotic inequality for bases of $H^0(X,mL)$ adapted to ordinary vanishing at $x$: their mean order divided by $m$ has lower limit greater than $n/(n+1)$ (Section <a href="#sec:min-ordinary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:min-ordinary">3</a>). We then minimize $$\frac{1}{h^0(X,mL)}\sum_i
 \exp\!\left(\frac{A(v)}{t}-\frac{v(s_i)}m\right),
 \qquad t<n+1,$$ over bases $(s_i)$ of $H^0(X,mL)$ and weighted monomial orders $v$ whose closed centers contain $x$. Here $A(v)$ is the log discrepancy, with the same scaling as $v$. This functional links three parts of the proof: its derivative under scaling detects the strict ordinary-order excess; its derivative in tangential directions measures a weighted first moment; and its gradient gives positive coefficients for the rational divisor used in the final lift.

Two obstacles enter this approach. First, both bases and models vary. Simultaneous lower bounds on normalized section orders can be encoded by an ideal on the product of $X$ and the complete flag variety of $H^0(X,mL)$. Threshold semicontinuity and retraction to one log resolution then give attainment of the minimum, without compactifying the space of all bases or all valuations (Section <a href="#sec:min-exponential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:min-exponential">4</a>). Second, a first variation in directions tangent to a positive-dimensional center gives an upper bound for a weighted moment of initial exponents. A uniform restriction-space estimate and Brunn–Minkowski give a contradictory lower bound. The uniformity is independent of the degree and singularities of the moving center (Section <a href="#sec:centers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:centers">5</a>).

The resulting point-centered minimizer determines a supporting functional on a finite collection of rational polytopes of normalized section orders. A small ideal perturbation makes discrepancy equality rigid along a reduced divisor $S$ over $x$. Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing then lifts a nonzero fiber value at $x$. Only local control near that equality locus is needed; the construction does not assume a globally log canonical boundary (Section <a href="#sec:isolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:isolation">6</a>). Figure <a href="#fig:proof-route" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-route">1</a> records the quantities passed between these steps.

<figure id="fig:proof-route">

<figcaption>The contradiction proving the sharp adjoint bound. The discrepancy bounds are uniform before fixing $t < n + 1$; for that one parameter, every minimizer has point center in arbitrarily large degrees. Here $S$ is the reduced divisor over $x$ on which the constructed boundary coefficient equals log discrepancy.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Orders, thresholds, and vanishing

Throughout the proof, $X$, $L$, and $n$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Write $$P=K_X+(n+1)L,\qquad R_j=H^0(X,jL),\qquad N_j=\dim R_j.$$ Ampleness gives <a id="eq:hilbert-X"></a>$$\label{eq:hilbert-X}
 N_j=\frac{L^n}{n!}j^n+O(j^{n-1}),\qquad L^n\geq1.$$ All centers are *closed* centers. Thus a divisor centered along a subvariety $W$ is an admissible test at every point of $W$, not just at its generic point.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Weighted orders

We use resolution and principalization in characteristic zero with a prescribed simple normal crossing (SNC) boundary; see (Włodarczyk 2005, Theorems 1.0.1 and 2.4.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[wlodarczyk2005](#ref-wlodarczyk2005)]. Models used for vanishing are projective. We may resolve above an already chosen model and retain any previously extracted prime by its strict transform.

For a prime divisor $F$ on a smooth birational model $\pi:Y\to X$, the log discrepancy is $$A(F)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(K_{Y/X}).$$ We permit positive multiples of $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F$ and scale discrepancy by the same multiple. Orders of sections mean orders of their effective zero divisors; orders of ideals mean the minimum of the orders of local generators. A local frame pulled back from $X$ may be used in these calculations.

<a id="eq:monomial-discrepancy"></a>
We also use weighted monomial orders. Choose a smooth projective birational model and local regular coordinates adapted to the *entire* relative canonical divisor: every component of that divisor meeting the coordinate neighborhood is a coordinate hyperplane. All coordinate divisors used below, together with that support, must form a simple normal crossing divisor. Choose a subset $E_1,\ldots,E_r$ of the coordinate divisors and give it positive real weights $u_1,\ldots,u_r$; the remaining coordinates have weight zero. The order of a germ is the least weight of a nonzero monomial in their local equations, with coefficients in the remaining coordinates, and $$\label{eq:monomial-discrepancy}
             A(v)=\sum_{j=1}^r u_jA(E_j).$$ A coordinate divisor absent from the relative canonical divisor has log discrepancy one. We use a chosen irreducible component of the positive-support stratum, whose generic point avoids all zero-weight coordinate divisors; its closed image is the center on $X$. This adaptation condition is essential for <a href="#eq:monomial-discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monomial-discrepancy">[eq:monomial-discrepancy]</a>. The test with all weights zero is excluded.

For positive rational weights this is a divisorial order up to scale. Indeed, clearing denominators and extracting the corresponding primitive ray is a toroidal modification in regular parameters. The relative canonical order in those parameters is the sum of the primitive weights minus one. Adding the pullback of the previous relative canonical divisor gives <a href="#eq:monomial-discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monomial-discrepancy">[eq:monomial-discrepancy]</a>. The extraction can be retained on a smooth projective model by resolution. Approximating weights on a fixed support by positive rational weights approximates both discrepancy and the orders of any fixed finite collection of sections. For the latter assertion only finitely many coordinatewise minimal exponents need be considered.

<a id="stmt-lem-monomial-local"></a>
<div id="lem:monomial-local" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Local computation of a monomial order). *A monomial order with fixed positive support can be computed in the Taylor ring at any smooth point of its open stratum. Its value is unchanged by replacing each positive-weight coordinate by a unit multiple or by changing the complementary zero-weight coordinates.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-monomial-local"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-monomial-local</summary>

*Proof.* For a positive threshold $c$, the corresponding ideal is generated by the monomials in the positive-weight parameters of weight at least $c$. These generators are preserved, up to units, under the stated changes. In the completed local ring the ideal is extended from a finite-colength monomial ideal in the positive-weight variables. It is therefore primary to their intersection. Localization at the generic point of that stratum followed by contraction does not change membership. The same conclusion in the regular local ring follows from faithful flatness of completion. In particular a coefficient which vanishes at the chosen closed point, but not identically on the stratum, is still a nonzero series in zero-weight variables; it does not raise the old weighted order. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-retraction"></a>
<div id="lem:retraction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (SNC retraction). *Let $D_1,\ldots,D_N$ be effective divisors on $X$, and let $Y\to X$ be a log resolution of their union. Include all exceptional divisors in its SNC divisor, with components $E_j$. For any positive multiple $v$ of a prime-divisor order over $Y$, set $u_j=v(E_j)$. Then <a id="eq:retraction"></a>$$\label{eq:retraction}
 v(D_i)=\sum_j u_j\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{E_j}(D_i),\qquad
 A(v)\geq\sum_j u_jA(E_j).$$ If some $u_j$ is nonzero, the monomial order with these weights at the stratum containing the generic center of $v$ has the same orders on the $D_i$, no larger discrepancy, and a closed center on $X$ containing that of $v$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-retraction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-retraction</summary>

*Proof.* Each pulled-back divisor is locally a monomial times a unit, which gives the first equality. The reduced SNC divisor on $Y$ is log canonical: equivalently, a logarithmic volume form has at most a simple pole along every prime on a higher smooth model. Thus the log discrepancy over $Y$ is at least $\sum_j u_j$. Adding the order of $K_{Y/X}$ gives the second inequality and the claimed discrepancy of the monomial order. The stratum closure contains the generic center, so its proper image contains the original closed center. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Thresholds in families

<a id="eq:lct-formula"></a>
For a coherent ideal $\mathfrak a$ on a smooth variety, its local log canonical threshold at $x$ is computed on a log resolution by $$\label{eq:lct-formula}
 \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_x(\mathfrak a)=
 \min_{x\in\pi(F),\,\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(\mathfrak a)>0}
       \frac{A(F)}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_F(\mathfrak a)}.$$ The minimum is $+\infty$ for the unit ideal. For the zero ideal the threshold is zero. The same formula follows by change of variables from local integrability of $(\sum |g_i|^2)^{-c}$ for generators $g_i$ of $\mathfrak a$.

<a id="stmt-lem-lct-family"></a>
<div id="lem:lct-family" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Closed threshold loci). *Let $V$ be a smooth complex projective variety, let $S$ be a complex algebraic variety, and let $\mathfrak a$ be an ideal on $V\times S$ locally given by algebraic families of generators. For a real number $c$, the locus $$\{(x,s):\mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_x(\mathfrak a_s)\leq c\}$$ is Zariski closed. Here $\mathfrak a_s$ denotes the ideal generated by the restricted generators, with no flatness requirement on its zero scheme.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lct-family"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lct-family</summary>

*Proof.* Locally embed the parameter space in a smooth space and extend the finitely many generators holomorphically to a coordinate neighborhood of that ambient space. The analytic semicontinuity of complex singularity exponents applies to $\varphi=\tfrac12\log\sum |g_i|^2$ there and hence on $S$; $e^{\varphi}$ is locally Hölder continuous. In local coordinates, translation of the variable handles a moving marked point. Consequently the displayed locus is closed in the classical topology; see (Demailly and Kollár 2001, Lemma 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[dk2001](#ref-dk2001)].

It is also algebraically constructible. The following generic-resolution stratification argument parallels the proof of (Demailly and Kollár 2001, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[dk2001](#ref-dk2001)]. Resolve the ideal over the generic point of each irreducible component of $S$ and spread the resolution over a dense open subset. After shrinking, generic smoothness makes the fibers and all relevant crossings smooth, so the fiber maps are log resolutions with fixed orders and discrepancy coefficients. Formula <a href="#eq:lct-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lct-formula">[eq:lct-formula]</a> expresses the threshold condition as a finite union of images of the relevant divisors on this piece. These images are algebraic because the resolution is proper. A finite cover used to separate components preserves constructibility of the image. Induction on the closed complement gives a finite stratification and proves constructibility on $V\times S$. The zero-ideal case is separated as an algebraic condition. In dimension zero the assertion follows directly from the vanishing of the generators.

A constructible subset of a complex algebraic variety which is classically closed is Zariski closed: every Zariski open subset of an irreducible closed subvariety is classically dense there. This proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Vanishing and descent

The cohomological input is Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing (Kawamata 1982; Viehweg 1982) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[kawamata1982](#ref-kawamata1982), [viehweg1982](#ref-viehweg1982)], in the following rounding formulation (Fujino 2009, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[fujinoKV](#ref-fujinoKV)].

<a id="stmt-thm-kv"></a>
<div id="thm:kv" class="theorem">

**Theorem 5** (Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing). *Let $T$ be smooth and projective over $\mathbb C$. Let $\Delta$ be an effective rational SNC divisor with coefficients in $[0,1)$, and let $D$ be an integral divisor. If $D-(K_T+\Delta)$ is nef and big, then $H^i(T,\mathcal O_T(D))=0$ for every $i>0$.*

</div>

This is the rounding formulation with $Q=D-K_T-\Delta$: its fractional support is SNC and $K_T+\left\lceil Q\right\rceil=D$. In particular, for an integral divisor $M$ and an effective rational SNC divisor $B$, nefness and bigness of $M-B$ give vanishing for $K_T+M-\left\lfloor B\right\rfloor$, by taking $\Delta=\{B\}$.

We will repeatedly use the following elementary descent observation.

<a id="stmt-lem-descent"></a>
<div id="lem:descent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $\pi:T\to V$ be a proper birational morphism of smooth varieties, let $P_V$ be a line bundle on $V$, and let $C$ be an integral divisor on $T$ whose positive part is exceptional. A section of $\pi^*P_V+C$ defines a regular section of $P_V$ on $V$. If a prime $F$ on $T$ maps to a point $x$, the coefficient of $C$ on $F$ is zero, and the section is nonzero generically on $F$, then the descended section is nonzero at $x$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-descent</summary>

*Proof.* View the section as a rational section of $P_V$ on the common function field. Its orders on all nonexceptional primes are nonnegative, since the coefficients of $C$ there are nonpositive. It is therefore regular in codimension one on the normal variety $V$, and hence everywhere. Under the additional assumptions its pullback has order zero along $F$. A local function vanishing at $x$ has positive order along every prime centered at $x$, so the descended section cannot vanish at $x$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:min-ordinary"></a>
### The ordinary-order alternative

This section converts failure of adjoint generation at a point into a strict asymptotic inequality for ordinary orders. Lattice counting gives the non-strict bound; the main task is to show that its equality case already permits a nonzero fiber value to be lifted.

Fix a closed point $x\in X$. Choose regular parameters at $x$ and a local frame of $L$, using its powers as frames of $jL$. We order Taylor monomials first by total degree and then lexicographically, always taking the smallest monomial. Let $$H_j=\{\operatorname{in}(s):0\ne s\in R_j\}
       \subset\mathbb Z_{\geq0}^n$$ be the set of initial exponents. Thus $|\operatorname{in}(s)|_1=\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)$.

The equality between the number of initial exponents and the dimension of the section space is the elementary counting principle in (Lazarsfeld and Mustaţă 2009, Lemma 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[lazarsfeldmustata2009](#ref-lazarsfeldmustata2009)] and (Kaveh and Khovanskii 2012, Proposition 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[kavehkhovanskii2012](#ref-kavehkhovanskii2012)]. We include its proof for the order chosen here.

<a id="stmt-lem-min-initials"></a>
<div id="lem:min-initials" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:min-multiplicity"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *The set $H_j$ has cardinality $N_j$, and representatives of its distinct exponents form a basis of $R_j$. There is a constant $C>0$, independent of $j$ and $s$, such that $$\label{eq:min-multiplicity}
 \mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)\leq Cj\qquad(0\ne s\in R_j).$$ Moreover $H_j+H_q\subset H_{j+q}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-min-initials"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-min-initials</summary>

*Proof.* Taylor expansion is injective on $R_j$: a section with zero germ vanishes on a nonempty open set and hence on the integral variety $X$. Each monomial coefficient has a one-dimensional space of possible values. Successively selecting a smallest initial monomial and eliminating its coefficient from the remaining basis vectors therefore produces a basis with distinct initials. Any linear combination of these vectors has the initial of its first nonzero summand in this ordering. This proves the cardinality assertion.

Choose a fixed sufficiently ample multiple $kL$. General members of its linear system through $x$ cut out a curve smooth at $x$ and not contained in the divisor of a prescribed nonzero section of $jL$. Local intersection multiplicity bounds $\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)$ from above by the degree of that divisor on the curve, at most $jk^{n-1}L^n$. The same assertion in dimension one is simply the degree bound for an effective divisor on $X$. This gives <a href="#eq:min-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-multiplicity">[eq:min-multiplicity]</a>. Finally, the chosen monomial ordering is multiplicative, so initial exponents add under products of sections. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-ordinary-gap"></a>
<div id="prop:ordinary-gap" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:min-gap"></a>
**Proposition 8** (Ordinary-order alternative). *If $P$ is not generated by its global sections at $x$, then $$\label{eq:min-gap}
 \liminf_{j\longrightarrow\infty}
   \frac{1}{jN_j}\sum_{\gamma\in H_j}|\gamma|_1
       >\frac{n}{n+1}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-ordinary-gap"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-ordinary-gap</summary>

*Proof.* We prove that failure of the strict inequality implies generation at $x$. The proof has three steps: lattice equality forces volume one, the resulting leading forms give a strict threshold on the exceptional divisor of the point blowup, and vanishing lifts a nonzero section from that divisor. Put $V=L^n$. The number of nonnegative integral points of total degree at most $r$ is $\binom{r+n}{n}$. Filling the points in order of increasing total degree, and using $N_j=Vj^n/n!+O(j^{n-1})$, gives <a id="eq:min-lattice-lower"></a>$$\label{eq:min-lattice-lower}
 \liminf_{j\longrightarrow\infty}
 \frac{1}{jN_j}\sum_{\gamma\in H_j}|\gamma|_1
 \geq V^{1/n}\frac{n}{n+1}.$$ Indeed the last occupied degree for a set of $N_j$ smallest points is $V^{1/n}j+O(1)$, and summing total degree over these points gives mean degree $V^{1/n}nj/(n+1)+O(1)$. Since $V$ is a positive integer, failure of <a href="#eq:min-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-gap">[eq:min-gap]</a> forces $V=1$ and equality along a subsequence.

Let $$\Delta=\{u\in\mathbb R_{\geq0}^n:|u|_1\leq1\}.$$ Along this subsequence, every fixed open ball with closure in the interior of $\Delta$ meets $j^{-1}H_j$ for all sufficiently large $j$. To see this, compare $H_j$ with a set $T_j$ of $N_j$ smallest lattice points. If such a ball were missed, at least $c_1j^n$ points of $T_j$ of degrees at most $(1-c_2)j$ would be absent, for fixed positive $c_1,c_2$. Every replacement outside $T_j$ has degree at least $j-O(1)$. The remaining replacements cannot decrease the degree sum, by the definition of $T_j$. The normalized mean would therefore exceed the minimum by a fixed positive amount, contradicting equality along the subsequence.

Choose a rational number $t'$ with $n<t'<n+1$ and put $c=(1/t',\ldots,1/t')$. This point is in the interior of $\Delta$. Choose finitely many small balls whose closures lie there and such that any choice of one point from each ball has convex hull containing $c$ in its interior. For one sufficiently large degree $j$ on the subsequence, choose initial exponents $\gamma_\nu\in H_j$ in these balls after scaling by $j^{-1}$. The rational polytope $$Q=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{\gamma_\nu/j\}$$ contains $c$ in its interior. Its intersection with $\{|u|_1=n/t'\}$ has rational points $p_1,\ldots,p_b$ whose convex hull contains $c$ in its interior relative to this full slice hyperplane. In dimension one this slice is the singleton $\{c\}$.

Write each $p_\ell$ as a rational convex combination of the $\gamma_\nu/j$, and choose sections $s_\nu$ with those initials. If $t'=a/b'$ in lowest terms, choose a positive integer $Q_0$ divisible by $a$ and by every denominator in these convex combinations. Taking the corresponding products of $Q_0$ sections gives sections $r_1,\ldots,r_b$ of $jQ_0L$. Set $h=jQ_0/t'$, which is a positive integer by construction. Their initial exponents $\beta_\ell$ satisfy <a id="eq:min-product-initials"></a>$$\label{eq:min-product-initials}
 r_\ell\in H^0(X,ht'L),\qquad
 \beta_\ell=jQ_0p_\ell,\qquad |\beta_\ell|_1=nh.$$ In particular, $\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{\beta_\ell/h\}$ contains $\mathbf1=(1,\ldots,1)$ in relative interior in the full hyperplane $\{|u|_1=n\}$.

<a id="eq:min-restricted-threshold"></a>
Let $p:B=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_xX\longrightarrow X$ and $E\simeq\mathbb P^{n-1}$ be the exceptional divisor. When $n=1$, take $p$ to be the identity and $E=x$. Removing the common order $nh$ from the pullbacks of the $r_\ell$ gives a subsystem of $$H=p^*(ht'L)-nhE$$ with base ideal $\mathfrak b$. Its restriction to $E$ is generated, in local frames, by the leading homogeneous forms $g_\ell$ of the $r_\ell$, each of degree $nh$. We claim that $$\label{eq:min-restricted-threshold}
 \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_z(E,\mathfrak b|_E)>1/h\qquad(z\in E).$$ Here the restricted ideal means its image in $\mathcal O_E$. For $n=1$ it is the unit ideal, so the assertion holds immediately.

The monomial threshold step below is the unit-monomial special case of Howald’s multiplier-ideal formula (Howald 2001, Main Theorem) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[howald2001](#ref-howald2001)]; we give the direct SNC argument in this setting.

For $n>1$, first replace the $g_\ell$ by their initial monomials $z^{\beta_\ell}$. On the affine chart of $E$ where $z_k\ne0$, their exponents are obtained by omitting coordinate $k$. Projection from the hyperplane $|u|_1=n$ is an affine isomorphism, so the resulting normalized exponent polytope contains the all-ones vector in ordinary interior. Consequently, for every nonzero $u\in\mathbb R_{\geq0}^{n-1}$, <a id="eq:min-monomial-strict"></a>$$\label{eq:min-monomial-strict}
 \frac1h\min_\ell\langle u,\beta_\ell^{(k)}\rangle
       <\sum_i u_i.$$ For a prime-divisor order over this chart, use its values on the coordinate functions as $u$. The left side is the order of the monomial ideal divided by $h$, while the right side is at most the log discrepancy, by the SNC inequality in Lemma <a href="#lem:retraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:retraction">3</a>. If $u=0$, the ideal order is zero and the discrepancy is positive. Thus the monomial ideal is klt at coefficient $1/h$. Computing its threshold on a log resolution, where a finite minimum suffices, gives a strict threshold greater than $1/h$ at every point.

This statement transfers to the original forms by an algebraic degeneration. Since only finitely many monomials occur, choose an integral weight vector $w$ which makes the lexicographic initial of each $g_\ell$ its unique term of smallest weight. The forms $$g_{\ell,\lambda}(z)
   =\lambda^{-\langle w,\beta_\ell\rangle}
      g_\ell(\lambda^{w_1}z_1,\ldots,\lambda^{w_n}z_n)$$ have polynomial coefficients in $\lambda$, with monomial special fiber. They define an algebraic family of ideals on $E\times\mathbb A^1$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:lct-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lct-family">4</a>, the locus where the fiber threshold is at most $1/h$ is closed. Its image in $\mathbb A^1$ is closed because $E$ is proper, and does not contain $0$. For some nonzero parameter the threshold is therefore greater than $1/h$ everywhere on $E$. All nonzero fibers are obtained from the original forms by a diagonal automorphism of $E$ and nonzero scalar changes of generators. This proves <a href="#eq:min-restricted-threshold" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-restricted-threshold">[eq:min-restricted-threshold]</a>. This application uses precisely the family semicontinuity of complex singularity exponents (Demailly and Kollár 2001) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[dk2001](#ref-dk2001)] incorporated in Lemma <a href="#lem:lct-family" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lct-family">4</a>.

It remains to lift from $E$. The blowup formula gives <a id="eq:min-adjoint-identity"></a>$$\label{eq:min-adjoint-identity}
 p^*P-(K_B+E)-H/h=(n+1-t')p^*L.$$ Take a projective log resolution $\sigma:T\longrightarrow B$ of $E$ and $\mathfrak b$, with strict transform $E'$ and $\mathfrak b\mathcal O_T=\mathcal O_T(-G)$. The divisor $E'$ is not a component of $G$, since the restricted system is not identically zero. Set $$D_T=K_T+\sigma^*(p^*P-K_B-E)-\lfloor G/h\rfloor.$$ This is an integral divisor, and <a id="eq:min-preliminary-positivity"></a>$$\label{eq:min-preliminary-positivity}
 D_T-K_T-\{G/h\}
 =(n+1-t')(p\sigma)^*L+(\sigma^*H-G)/h.$$ The first summand is nef and big and the second is nef, because $\sigma^*H-G$ is globally generated. The fractional boundary has SNC support. Theorem <a href="#thm:kv" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:kv">5</a>, in the stated logarithmic form of Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing (Fujino 2009) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[fujinoKV](#ref-fujinoKV)], therefore gives $H^1(T,\mathcal O_T(D_T))=0$.

<a id="eq:min-preliminary-restriction"></a>
Let $\sigma_E:E'\longrightarrow E$ be the induced morphism. Adjunction identifies the restriction of $D_T+E'$ with $$\label{eq:min-preliminary-restriction}
 \sigma_E^*(p^*P|_E)
   +K_{E'/E}-\lfloor G|_{E'}/h\rfloor.$$ Indeed $E'+\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(G)$ is SNC. Each component restricts with multiplicity one, and two distinct components cannot restrict to the same prime divisor on $E'$, so taking floors commutes with restriction. The generators on $E'$ generate $\mathcal O_{E'}(-G|_{E'})$, the pullback of the restricted ideal. The strict threshold in <a href="#eq:min-restricted-threshold" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-restricted-threshold">[eq:min-restricted-threshold]</a> implies $$K_{E'/E}-\lfloor G|_{E'}/h\rfloor\geq0.$$ The first line bundle in <a href="#eq:min-preliminary-restriction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-preliminary-restriction">[eq:min-preliminary-restriction]</a> is trivial with fiber $P_x$. A nonzero vector in this fiber, multiplied by the canonical section of the effective correction, gives a section on $E'$ nonzero at its generic point. The exact sequence for $E'$ and the vanishing just proved lift it to a section of $D_T+E'$.

Relative to $\sigma^*p^*P$, the correction of this lifted bundle is $$C_T=K_{T/B}-\sigma^*E+E'-\lfloor G/h\rfloor.$$ Its coefficient on $E'$ is zero, and its coefficients on every other $\sigma$-nonexceptional divisor are nonpositive. Its positive part is therefore exceptional over $X$. Since $E'$ maps to $x$ and the lifted section is nonzero at its generic point, Lemma <a href="#lem:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:descent">6</a>, applied to $p\sigma$, gives a section of $P$ nonzero at $x$. This proves the proposition, including dimension one, when $E'$ is the point itself. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:min-exponential"></a>
### Finite-degree exponential minimization

We now allow both the basis and the monomial test to vary. The strict ordinary-order inequality makes the objective less than one, and a supply of low-order jets bounds the discrepancy on that sublevel set. To prove attainment, we first select one flag and its adapted basis; only then do we choose the resolution on which compactness is used.

<a id="eq:min-objective"></a>
For $N_m>0$, an ordered basis $\mathbf s=(s_1,\ldots,s_{N_m})$ of $R_m$, a nonzero monomial test $v$, and $t>0$, put $$\label{eq:min-objective}
 F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s)
  =\frac1{N_m}\sum_{i=1}^{N_m}
       \exp\!\left(\frac{A(v)}t-\frac{v(s_i)}m\right),
 \qquad
 f_{m,t}(z)=\inf_{\substack{v,\mathbf s\\z\in c_X(v)}}
                F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s).$$ Here $c_X(v)$ denotes the closed center, and the infimum also ranges over the bases. Positive rescaling of a test is always allowed. Approximating its positive weights by rational weights preserves its center and approximates the orders of finitely many sections and its discrepancy. Thus the same infimum is obtained using positive multiples of prime-divisor orders.

<a id="stmt-lem-min-order-bound"></a>
<div id="lem:min-order-bound" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:min-order-bound"></a>
**Lemma 9**. *If the center of a test $v$ contains $x$, then every nonzero section $s$ satisfies $$\label{eq:min-order-bound}
 v(s)\leq A(v)\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s).$$ In particular, for $s\in R_m$ one has $0\leq v(s)/A(v)\leq Cm$, with $C$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:min-initials" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:min-initials">7</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-min-order-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-min-order-bound</summary>

*Proof.* For an effective divisor with local equation $g$ of multiplicity $r>0$ at a smooth point, the elementary bound $\mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_x(g)\geq1/r$ suffices. One analytic proof uses Weierstrass preparation in a general coordinate direction. On a smaller polydisc, $g$ is a unit times a monic polynomial of degree $r$ in that coordinate. For any $c<1/r$, factor this polynomial into its $r$ complex roots for each fixed choice of the other coordinates. Hölder’s inequality bounds the integral of its absolute value to the power $-2c$ by the product of integrals with exponents $-2cr$. These one-variable integrals are uniformly finite because $cr<1$ and the roots remain bounded. Fubini’s theorem proves local integrability. The unit case has infinite threshold. Computing the threshold on a log resolution now gives <a href="#eq:min-order-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-order-bound">[eq:min-order-bound]</a> for divisorial orders with centers containing $x$, and rational approximation gives it for the admitted monomial tests. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-min-coercivity"></a>
<div id="lem:min-coercivity" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:min-uniform-bounds"></a>
**Lemma 10** (Uniform sublevel bounds). *Suppose that $P$ is not generated at $x$. There are numbers $0<t_0<n+1$, $c>0$, $0<\delta<1$, $0<a_*\leq a^*<\infty$, and an integer $m_0$ such that, for every $m\geq m_0$ and every $t\in[t_0,n+1]$, $$\label{eq:min-uniform-bounds}
 c\leq f_{m,t}(x)\leq1-\delta.$$ Moreover, any pair in <a href="#eq:min-objective" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-objective">[eq:min-objective]</a> centered with image containing $x$ and satisfying $F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s)\leq1-\delta/2$ has <a id="eq:min-discrepancy-bounds"></a>$$\label{eq:min-discrepancy-bounds}
 a_*\leq A(v)\leq a^*.$$ All these constants are independent of $m$ and $t$ in the indicated ranges.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-min-coercivity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-min-coercivity</summary>

*Proof.* By Proposition <a href="#prop:ordinary-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ordinary-gap">8</a>, there is $\epsilon>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $m$, $$\frac1{mN_m}\sum_{\gamma\in H_m}|\gamma|_1
       \geq\frac n{n+1}+3\epsilon.$$ Choose $t_0<n+1$ sufficiently close that $n/t_0\leq n/(n+1)+\epsilon$. Take a basis with distinct ordinary initials, and use $v=\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x$, where $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x$ is the ordinary point order and $A(\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x)=n$. In dimension one this is the order of the point itself. The linear coefficient at $\lambda=0$ in its objective is at most $-2\epsilon$, uniformly in $m$ and $t$. By <a href="#eq:min-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-multiplicity">[eq:min-multiplicity]</a>, the quantities $n/t-\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s_i)/m$ are uniformly bounded, so the quadratic remainder in their exponential expansion is uniformly $O(\lambda^2)$. A fixed sufficiently small $\lambda>0$ therefore gives $F_{m,t}\leq1-\delta$ for a fixed $\delta>0$.

<a id="eq:min-jet-codimension"></a>
We next give a uniform supply of low-order jets. Choose $k>0$ such that $kL$ is very ample, and sections $s_0,s_1,\ldots,s_n$ of $kL$ such that $s_0(x)\ne0$ and $z_i=s_i/s_0$ are regular parameters at $x$. For each residue class modulo $k$, choose a fixed sufficiently large integer $b_r$ in that class for which $b_rL$ is globally generated, and choose $\tau_r\in H^0(X,b_rL)$ nonzero at $x$. Write $m=qk+b_r$ using the corresponding residue. For $|\alpha|_1\leq q$, the sections $$\tau_r s_0^{q-|\alpha|_1}s_1^{\alpha_1}\cdots s_n^{\alpha_n}$$ have germs equal to a common unit times $z^\alpha$ in a local frame of $mL$. In particular, if $$r_m=\min\left(q,\left\lfloor\frac{m}{2(n+1)}\right\rfloor\right),$$ their jets for $|\alpha|_1\leq r_m$ are linearly independent modulo ordinary order greater than $m/(2t)$. For large $m$, $r_m$ is at least a fixed positive multiple of $m$. The Hilbert asymptotic consequently gives a constant $0<\kappa<1$, independent of $m,t$, with $$\label{eq:min-jet-codimension}
 \mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_{R_m}\{s:\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(s)>m/(2t)\}\geq\kappa N_m.$$

Put $a=A(v)$. By Lemma <a href="#lem:min-order-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:min-order-bound">9</a>, the subspace $\{s:v(s)>ma/(2t)\}$ is contained in the subspace in <a href="#eq:min-jet-codimension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-jet-codimension">[eq:min-jet-codimension]</a>. Thus at least $\kappa N_m$ vectors of any basis, whether or not it is adapted to $v$, have $v(s_i)\leq ma/(2t)$. It follows that <a id="eq:min-coercive-exponential"></a>$$\label{eq:min-coercive-exponential}
 F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s)
   \geq\kappa\exp\!\left(\frac a{2t}\right)
   \geq\kappa\exp\!\left(\frac a{2(n+1)}\right).$$ This proves the lower bound in <a href="#eq:min-uniform-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-uniform-bounds">[eq:min-uniform-bounds]</a>, with $c=\kappa$, and bounds $a$ above on the stated sublevel set.

Finally, <a href="#eq:min-order-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-order-bound">[eq:min-order-bound]</a> and <a href="#eq:min-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-multiplicity">[eq:min-multiplicity]</a> show that $F_{m,t}(v,\mathbf s)\geq e^{-Ca}$. Choose $a_*>0$ sufficiently small that $e^{-Ca_*}>1-\delta/2$. No pair in the sublevel set can have $a<a_*$. Enlarging the upper bound if necessary gives $a^*\geq a_*$ and proves all the assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-min-flags"></a>
<div id="lem:min-flags" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:min-rational-list"></a>
**Lemma 11** (Closed realizability loci). *Fix $m$ with $N_m>0$ and a decreasing list $\alpha_1\geq\cdots\geq\alpha_{N_m}\geq0$ of rational numbers. The set of points $z\in X$ for which some ordered basis and some prime-divisor order $v$, with $z\in c_X(v)$, satisfy $$\label{eq:min-rational-list}
 \frac{v(s_i)}{A(v)}\geq\alpha_i
       \qquad(1\leq i\leq N_m)$$ is Zariski closed. There is also a closed incidence locus in the product of $X$ with the complete flag variety of $R_m$ recording realizability by bases adapted to a given flag.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-min-flags"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-min-flags</summary>

<a id="eq:min-flag-inequalities"></a>
*Proof.* Let $\mathcal F$ be that projective flag variety. A flag has subspaces $U_i\subset R_m$ of dimensions $i$ and associated base ideals $\mathfrak b_i\subset\mathcal O_X$, obtained by evaluation after twisting by $-mL$. If <a href="#eq:min-rational-list" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:min-rational-list">[eq:min-rational-list]</a> holds, the flag spanned by the first $i$ basis vectors satisfies $$\label{eq:min-flag-inequalities}
 v(\mathfrak b_i)\geq\alpha_iA(v)
